Formally,
\mathsf{PhaseEC}
is defined as the following program:

\mathsf{PhaseEC}^{\textsf{{a}},\textsf{{b}}}[\texttt{x},\texttt{y},\texttt{z}]\ \ \triangleq\ \ \mathsf{H}[\texttt{x}];\,\mathsf{H}[\texttt{y}];\,\mathsf{H}[\texttt{z}];\,\mathsf{BitEC}^{\textsf{{a}},\textsf{{b}}}[\texttt{x},\texttt{y},\texttt{z}];\,\mathsf{H}[\texttt{x}];\,\mathsf{H}[\texttt{y}];\,\mathsf{H}[\texttt{z}].

We can immediately derive the following specification of the procedure
\mathsf{PhaseEC}
from the specification of
\mathsf{BitEC}
, for any
e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}\in\{0,1\}
such that
e_{1}+e_{2}+e_{3}\leq 1
:

\displaystyle\forall\mkern 1.0mu\alpha,\beta.\allowbreak

\displaystyle\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\mkern 2.0mu(\texttt{x},\texttt{y},\texttt{z})\mapsto\mathsf{Z}^{e_{1}}\mathsf{Z}^{e_{2}}\mathsf{Z}^{e_{3}}(\alpha\left|\mathord{+}\mathord{+}\mathord{+}\right\rangle+\beta\left|\mathord{-}\mathord{-}\mathord{-}\right\rangle)\mkern 2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}^{\textsf{{a}},\textsf{{b}}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{PhaseEC}^{\textsf{{a}},\textsf{{b}}}[\texttt{x},\texttt{y},\texttt{z}]\hskip 10.00002pt\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\mkern 2.0mu(\texttt{x},\texttt{y},\texttt{z})\mapsto\lparen\alpha\left|\mathord{+}\mathord{+}\mathord{+}\right\rangle+\beta\left|\mathord{-}\mathord{-}\mathord{-}\right\rangle\rparen\,*\,P^{\,\mathsf{BitEC}^{\textsf{{a}},\textsf{{b}}}}_{e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}}\mkern 2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}.

B.6.
Error Correction: Shor Code

Here we give the details of
Section

5.5
.

Now, we can combine the bit-flip code and the phase-flip code to construct a universal quantum error correction code capable of correcting any single-qubit error.
This construction is known as the
Shor code
, which encodes a single logical qubit into 9 physical qubits.
The Shor code achieves this by nesting two types of codes: we first apply a phase-flip code to the logical qubit, and then apply a bit-flip code to each of the resulting qubits.
Concretely, the Shor code encodes the logical qubit state
\alpha\left|0\right\rangle+\beta\left|1\right\rangle
into

\alpha\left|0_{\mathsf{L}}0_{\mathsf{L}}0_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle+\beta\left|1_{\mathsf{L}}1_{\mathsf{L}}1_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle,

where
\left|0_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle
and
\left|1_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle
are phase-flip-encoded states.
In this way, the Shor code uses a bit-flip code over logical qubits that have already been protected against phase-flip errors, ensuring robustness against both bit-flip and phase-flip errors.

Such codes that can correct both bit-flip and phase-flip errors are universal in the sense that they can correct any single-qubit unitary error.
The sketch of the intuitive proof of this is as follows.
Because the Pauli gates
\mathsf{id}
,
\mathsf{X}
,
\mathsf{Z}
and
\mathsf{Y}=-\sqrt{-1}\mkern 2.0mu\mathsf{Z}\mathsf{X}
form an orthogonal basis for the whole linear space of complex
2\times 2
matrices,
any single-qubit unitary error
U
can be decomposed into a linear combination of
\mathsf{id}
,
\mathsf{X}
,
\mathsf{Z}
and
\mathsf{Z}\mathsf{X}
, with a coefficient vector of norm
1
.
So we can write
U=\lambda_{0}\mathsf{id}+\lambda_{1}\mathsf{X}+\lambda_{2}\mathsf{Z}+\lambda_{3}\mathsf{Z}\mathsf{X}
for some
(\lambda_{i})_{i=0}^{3}
such that
\sum_{i=0}^{3}\lvert\lambda_{i}\rvert^{2}=1
.
Note that a state
\left|\psi\right\rangle
with the error
U
applied,
U\left|\psi\right\rangle
, can be expressed as

\lambda_{0}\left|\psi\right\rangle+\lambda_{1}\mathsf{X}\left|\psi\right\rangle+\lambda_{2}\mathsf{Z}\left|\psi\right\rangle+\lambda_{3}\mathsf{Z}\mathsf{X}\left|\psi\right\rangle.

By linearity of quantum computation,
it suffices to show that the matrices
\mathsf{X}
,
\mathsf{Z}
and
\mathsf{Z}\mathsf{X}
, can be corrected by the Shor code.
Perhaps surprisingly, this is immediate,
because
\mathsf{PhaseEC}
and
\mathsf{BitEC}_{\mathsf{L}}
are designed to correct
\mathsf{X}
and
\mathsf{Z}
errors, respectively.

\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{e_{1}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{U}

\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{e_{1}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{U}

\mathsf{PhaseEC}

\mathsf{PhaseEC}

\mathsf{BitEC}_{\mathsf{L}}

\mathsf{BitEC}_{\mathsf{L}}

\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{e_{2}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{U}

\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{e_{2}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{U}

\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{e_{3}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{U}

\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{e_{3}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{U}

\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{e_{4}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{U}

\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{e_{4}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{U}

\mathsf{PhaseEC}

\mathsf{PhaseEC}

\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{e_{5}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{U}

\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{e_{5}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{U}

\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{e_{6}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{U}

\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{e_{6}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{U}

\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{e_{7}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{U}

\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{e_{7}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{U}

\mathsf{PhaseEC}

\mathsf{PhaseEC}

\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{e_{8}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{U}

\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{e_{8}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{U}

\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{e_{9}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{U}

\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{e_{9}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{U}

Figure 22.
Error correction circuit for the Shor code.

The Shor code is a perfect example to demonstrate the power of our separation logic.
The error correction circuit
\mathsf{ShorEC}
for the Shor code is given in
Fig.

22

and the formal definition has been given in
Section

5.5
.
As we can see, the whole circuit has 9 qubits, meaning the dimension of the Hilbert space is
2^{9}=512
.
However, it can be decomposed into smaller modules,
which can be verified independently using separation logic.
This not only makes the verification process more manageable,
it also lets us use our linear-combination rule to simplify the proof,
since the universality of the Shor code is based on the linearity of quantum computation as sketched above.

Let us first explain how the last component
\mathsf{BitEC}_{\mathsf{L}}
is constructed.
This program is essentially the same as the 3-qubit bit-flip code
\mathsf{BitEC}
we have already verified,
but with each physical qubit replaced by a logical qubit encoded by the phase-flip code.
Since we used
\mathsf{X}
and
\mathsf{CNOT}
gates and a measurement to implement the bit-flip code,
we first implement these gates for the phase-flip code.
That is, we verify the following assertions for programs
\mathsf{X}_{\mathsf{L}}
,

\mathsf{CX}_{\mathsf{L}}
and
\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{Z}}^{\mathsf{L}}
to use them as building blocks for the error correction of the bit-flip code.

\displaystyle\exists\mkern 1.0mu\,Q\mkern 3.0mu\mathpunct{:}\allowbreak\mkern 3.0mu\mathsf{frameable},\mathop{\mathsf{prob}}1.\allowbreak\,\ \forall\mkern 1.0mui\in\{0,1\}.\allowbreak\,\

\displaystyle\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\bar{\texttt{x}}\mapsto\left|i_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}\mkern 5.0mu{\mathsf{X}_{\mathsf{L}}[\bar{\texttt{x}}]}\mkern 5.0mu\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\bar{\texttt{x}}\mapsto\left|\neg i_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle*Q}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}

\displaystyle\exists\mkern 1.0mu\,Q\mkern 3.0mu\mathpunct{:}\allowbreak\mkern 3.0mu\mathsf{frameable},\mathop{\mathsf{prob}}1.\allowbreak\,\ \forall\mkern 1.0mui,j\in\{0,1\}.\allowbreak\,\

\displaystyle\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}})\mapsto\left|i_{\mathsf{L}}j_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}\mkern 5.0mu{\mathsf{CX}_{\mathsf{L}}[\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}}]}\mkern 5.0mu\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}})\mapsto\left|i_{\mathsf{L}}(i\veebar\mkern-1.0muj)_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle\mkern 1.0mu*\mkern 1.0muQ}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}

\displaystyle\exists\mkern 1.0mu\,Q\mkern 3.0mu\mathpunct{:}\allowbreak\mkern 3.0mu\mathsf{frameable},\mathop{\mathsf{prob}}1.\allowbreak\,\

\displaystyle\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\bar{\texttt{x}}\mapsto\left|0_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}^{\textsf{{a}}}\mkern 5.0mu{\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{Z}}^{\mathsf{L}\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{{a}}}[\bar{\texttt{x}}]}\mkern 5.0mu\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\big\lparen\mkern 1.0mu\bar{\texttt{x}}\mapsto\left|0_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle\,\mathbin{\prescript{}{\text{\scalebox{.8}{$0$}}\mkern-2.5mu}{\oplus}^{\textsf{{a}}}_{\mkern-1.5mu\text{\scalebox{.8}{$1$}}}}\,\bar{\texttt{x}}\mapsto 0\mkern 1.0mu\big\rparen\mkern 2.0mu*\mkern 2.0muQ}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}

\displaystyle\exists\mkern 1.0mu\,Q\mkern 3.0mu\mathpunct{:}\allowbreak\mkern 3.0mu\mathsf{frameable},\mathop{\mathsf{prob}}1.\allowbreak\,\

\displaystyle\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\bar{\texttt{x}}\mapsto\left|1_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}^{\textsf{{a}}}\mkern 5.0mu{\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{Z}}^{\mathsf{L}\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{{a}}}[\bar{\texttt{x}}]}\mkern 5.0mu\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\big\lparen\mkern 1.0mu\bar{\texttt{x}}\mapsto 0\,\mathbin{\prescript{}{\text{\scalebox{.8}{$0$}}\mkern-2.5mu}{\oplus}^{\textsf{{a}}}_{\mkern-1.5mu\text{\scalebox{.8}{$1$}}}}\,\bar{\texttt{x}}\mapsto\left|0_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle\mkern 1.0mu\big\rparen\mkern 2.0mu*\mkern 2.0muQ}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}

Notably, we can abstract the factor
Q
by the condition
Q\mkern 3.0mu\mathpunct{:}\allowbreak\mkern 3.0mu\mathsf{frameable},\mathop{\mathsf{prob}}1
.
Then, we can replace the basic gates
\mathsf{X}
,
\mathsf{CX}
and
\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{Z}}
in
\mathsf{BitEC}

with the above programs to implement
\mathsf{BitEC}_{\mathsf{L}}
.
More concretely,
\mathsf{BitEC}_{\mathsf{L}}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{BitEC}_{\mathsf{L}}^{\textsf{{a}},\textsf{{b}}}[\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}}]\ \ \triangleq

\displaystyle\mathsf{CX}_{\mathsf{L}}[\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{x}}];\,\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{Z}}^{\mathsf{L}\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{{a}}}[\bar{\texttt{x}}];\,\mathsf{CX}_{\mathsf{L}}[\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{x}}];\,\mathsf{CX}_{\mathsf{L}}[\bar{\texttt{z}},\bar{\texttt{y}}];\,\mathsf{M}_{\mathsf{Z}}^{\mathsf{L}\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{{b}}}[\bar{\texttt{y}}];\,\mathsf{CX}_{\mathsf{L}}[\bar{\texttt{z}},\bar{\texttt{y}}];

\displaystyle\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{\textsf{{a}}\land\neg\textsf{{b}}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{\mathsf{X}_{\mathsf{L}}[\bar{\texttt{x}}]};\,\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{\textsf{{a}}\land\textsf{{b}}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{\mathsf{X}_{\mathsf{L}}[\bar{\texttt{y}}]};\,\mathsf{if}\mkern 6.0mu{\neg\textsf{{a}}\land\textsf{{b}}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{then}\mkern 6.0mu{\mathsf{X}_{\mathsf{L}}[\bar{\texttt{z}}]}.

We can also verify the correctness of
\mathsf{BitEC}_{\mathsf{L}}

exactly as we have done for the normal bit-flip code.

\displaystyle\exists\mkern 1.0mu\,Q\mkern 3.0mu\mathpunct{:}\allowbreak\mkern 3.0mu\mathsf{frameable},\mathop{\mathsf{prob}}1.\allowbreak\,\,\ \forall\mkern 1.0mue_{1},e_{2},e_{3}\mathrel{\text{{s.t.}}}e_{1}+e_{2}+e_{3}\leq 1.\allowbreak\,\,\ \forall\mkern 1.0mu\alpha,\beta.\allowbreak\,\hskip 10.00002pt

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\mkern 2.0mu(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto(\mathsf{X}_{\mathsf{L}}^{e_{1}}\mathsf{X}_{\mathsf{L}}^{e_{2}}\mathsf{X}_{\mathsf{L}}^{e_{3}})(\alpha\left|0_{\mathsf{L}}0_{\mathsf{L}}0_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle+\beta\left|1_{\mathsf{L}}1_{\mathsf{L}}1_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle)\mkern 2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}^{\textsf{{a}},\textsf{{b}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt\mathsf{BitEC}_{\mathsf{L}}^{\textsf{{a}},\textsf{{b}}}[\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}}]\hskip 10.00002pt\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\mkern 2.0mu(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto\lparen\alpha\left|0_{\mathsf{L}}0_{\mathsf{L}}0_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle+\beta\left|1_{\mathsf{L}}1_{\mathsf{L}}1_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle\rparen\,*\,P^{\,\mathsf{BitEC}^{\textsf{{a}},\textsf{{b}}}}_{e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}}\,*\,Q\mkern 2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}

This procedure of lifting the proof of correctness of the 3-qubit bit-flip code
to the 9-qubit version
\mathsf{BitEC}_{\mathsf{L}}
is not completely automatic.
However, we can easily redo the proof of correctness of a generalized
bit-flip code implemented on top of any logical qubit encoded by an arbitrary code.
This allows us to reuse the specification of the bit-flip code
as a building block in the verification of any program that uses it.
In this way, we can still avoid verifying the correctness of the bit-flip code
every time we use it.

Finally, we verify the correctness of the Shor code.
The specification of the correctness is similar to the ones we have seen (we omit classical variables for simplicity):

\displaystyle\textstyle\exists\mkern 1.0mu\,P\mkern 3.0mu\mathpunct{:}\allowbreak\mkern 3.0mu\mathsf{frameable},\mathop{\mathsf{prob}}1.\allowbreak\,\ \forall\mkern 1.0mu\alpha,\beta.\allowbreak\,\hskip 8.00003pt

\displaystyle\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\,(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto(U^{e_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes U^{e_{9}})\left\lparen\alpha\left|0_{\mathsf{L}}0_{\mathsf{L}}0_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle+\beta\left|1_{\mathsf{L}}1_{\mathsf{L}}1_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle\right\rparen\,}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 13.99995pt\mathsf{ShorEC}[\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}}]\hskip 8.00003pt\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\,(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto\lparen\alpha\left|0_{\mathsf{L}}0_{\mathsf{L}}0_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle+\beta\left|1_{\mathsf{L}}1_{\mathsf{L}}1_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle\rparen\,*\,P\,}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}

This holds for any single-qubit unitary error
U
and any
e_{1},\ldots,e_{9}\in\{0,1\}
under
\sum_{i=1}^{9}e_{i}=1
.
18
18

18

We can safely exclude the case where
e_{1}=\cdots=e_{9}=0
, because we can set
U=\mathrm{id}
to model the error-free situation.

To simplify the proof, we first assume the case
where
U=\mathsf{Z}^{a}\mathsf{X}^{b}
for
a,b\in\{0,1\}
.
We can derive the following on
\mathsf{PhaseEC}[\bar{\texttt{x}}]
.

\frac{\frac{\textstyle\forall\mkern 1.0muc\in\{0,1\}.\allowbreak\,\ \big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\bar{\texttt{x}}\mapsto(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{3}\mathsf{Z}^{ae_{i}}\mathsf{X}^{be_{i}})\left|c_{\mathsf{L}}c_{\mathsf{L}}c_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}\hskip 9.24994pt\mathsf{PhaseEC}[\bar{\texttt{x}}]\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 129.49915pt\textstyle\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\,\bar{\texttt{x}}\mapsto(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{3}\mathsf{X}^{be_{i}})\left|c_{\mathsf{L}}c_{\mathsf{L}}c_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle\,*\,P^{\,\mathsf{BitEC}}_{ae_{1},ae_{2},ae_{3}}\,}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}}{\textstyle\forall\mkern 1.0muc\in\{0,1\}.\allowbreak\,\ \big\{\mkern 2.0mu{(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{9}\mathsf{Z}^{ae_{i}}\mathsf{X}^{be_{i}})\left|c_{\mathsf{L}}c_{\mathsf{L}}c_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}\hskip 9.24994pt\mathsf{PhaseEC}[\bar{\texttt{x}}]\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 83.24945pt\textstyle\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\,(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{3}\mathsf{X}^{be_{i}}\otimes\bigotimes_{i=4}^{9}\mathsf{Z}^{ae_{i}}\mathsf{X}^{be_{i}})\left|c_{\mathsf{L}}c_{\mathsf{L}}c_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle\,*\,P^{\,\mathsf{BitEC}}_{ae_{1},ae_{2},ae_{3}}\,}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Right = \ref{lrule:hoare-frame}}}{\textstyle\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{9}\mathsf{Z}^{ae_{i}}\mathsf{X}^{be_{i}})(\alpha\left|0_{\mathsf{L}}0_{\mathsf{L}}0_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle+\beta\left|1_{\mathsf{L}}1_{\mathsf{L}}1_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle)}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}\hskip 9.24994pt\mathsf{PhaseEC}[\bar{\texttt{x}}]\\
\hskip 27.74982pt\textstyle\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\,(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{3}\mathsf{X}^{be_{i}}\otimes\bigotimes_{i=4}^{9}\mathsf{Z}^{ae_{i}}\mathsf{X}^{be_{i}})(\alpha\left|0_{\mathsf{L}}0_{\mathsf{L}}0_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle+\beta\left|1_{\mathsf{L}}1_{\mathsf{L}}1_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle)\,*\,P^{\,\mathsf{BitEC}}_{ae_{1},ae_{2},ae_{3}}\,}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right = \ref{lrule:hoare-sum}}

Here, we used
ae_{1}+ae_{2}+ae_{3}\leq 1
.
Using this kind of derivation for
\mathsf{PhaseEC}
three times and the specification of the enriched bit-flip code
\mathsf{BitEC}
, we can derive the following for any
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{C}
,
letting
\left|\psi_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle\triangleq\alpha\left|0_{\mathsf{L}}0_{\mathsf{L}}0_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle+\beta\left|1_{\mathsf{L}}1_{\mathsf{L}}1_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle
:

\displaystyle\textstyle\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{9}\mathsf{Z}^{ae_{i}}\mathsf{X}^{be_{i}})\left|\psi_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}

\displaystyle\mathsf{PhaseEC}[\bar{\texttt{x}}]

\displaystyle\textstyle\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{3}\mathsf{X}^{be_{i}}\otimes\bigotimes_{i=4}^{9}\mathsf{Z}^{ae_{i}}\mathsf{X}^{be_{i}})\left|\psi_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle\,*\,P^{(1)}_{a}}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}

\displaystyle\mathsf{PhaseEC}[\bar{\texttt{y}}]

\displaystyle\textstyle\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{6}\mathsf{X}^{be_{i}}\otimes\bigotimes_{i=7}^{9}\mathsf{Z}^{ae_{i}}\mathsf{X}^{be_{i}})\left|\psi_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle\,*\,P^{(2)}_{a}}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}

\displaystyle\mathsf{PhaseEC}[\bar{\texttt{z}}]

\displaystyle\textstyle\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{9}\mathsf{X}^{be_{i}})\left|\psi_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle\,*\,P^{(3)}_{a}}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}

\displaystyle\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto(\mathsf{X}^{b(e_{1}\lor e_{2}\lor e_{3})}_{\mathsf{L}}\otimes\mathsf{X}^{b(e_{4}\lor e_{5}\lor e_{6})}_{\mathsf{L}}\otimes\mathsf{X}^{b(e_{7}\lor e_{8}\lor e_{9})}_{\mathsf{L}})\left|\psi_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle\,*\,P^{(3)}_{a}}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}

\displaystyle\mathsf{BitEC}_{\mathsf{L}}[\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}}]

\displaystyle\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto\left|\psi_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle\,*\,P^{(4)}_{a,b}}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}

Note that every
P^{(k)}
is a global phase guard that satisfies

P^{(k)}\mkern 3.0mu\mathpunct{:}\allowbreak\mkern 3.0mu\mathsf{frameable}
, thanks to the fact that the separating conjunction of frameable assertions is frameable (
Fig.

18
).
Importantly, each
P^{(k)}
depends on the error parameters
a,b
(and each
e_{i}
),
but it does not depend on the initial state
\left|\psi_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle
.
Finally, we can use the linear combination rule to derive the final specification of the Shor code.
Let a single-qubit error
U
be written as
\sum_{a,b\in\{0,1\}}\lambda_{a,b}\mathsf{Z}^{a}\mathsf{X}^{b}
for
\lparen\lambda_{a,b}\rparen_{a,b}
such that
\sum_{a,b\in\{0,1\}}\lvert\lambda_{a,b}\rvert^{2}=1
.
Then we have the following derivation (we here abbreviate
\mathsf{ShorEC}[\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}}]
as
\mathsf{ShorEC}
):

\frac{\frac{\textstyle\forall\mkern 1.0mua,b.\allowbreak\,\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{9}\mathsf{Z}^{ae_{i}}\mathsf{X}^{be_{i}})\left|\psi_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}\mkern 5.0mu{\mathsf{ShorEC}}\mkern 5.0mu\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto\left|\psi_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle\mkern 1.0mu*\mkern 1.0muP^{(4)}_{a,b}}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}}{\forall\mkern 1.0mua,b.\allowbreak\,\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{9}\mathsf{Z}^{ae_{i}}\mathsf{X}^{be_{i}})\lambda_{a,b}\left|\psi_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}\mkern 5.0mu{\mathsf{ShorEC}}\mkern 5.0mu\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto\left|\psi_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle\mkern 1.0mu*\mkern 1.0mu\lambda_{a,b}P^{(4)}_{a,b}}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}}\hskip 8.04996pt\textsc{Right = \ref{lrule:hoare-scale}}}{\textstyle\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto\sum_{a,b}(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{9}\mathsf{Z}^{ae_{i}}\mathsf{X}^{be_{i}})\lambda_{a,b}\left|\psi_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}\mkern 5.0mu{\mathsf{ShorEC}}\mkern 5.0mu\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{(\bar{\texttt{x}},\bar{\texttt{y}},\bar{\texttt{z}})\mapsto\left|\psi_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle\mkern 1.0mu*\mkern 1.0mu\sum_{a,b}\lambda_{a,b}P^{(4)}_{a,b}}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Right = \ref{lrule:hoare-sum}}

The precondition can be rewritten as

\sum_{a,b}(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{9}\mathsf{Z}^{ae_{i}}\mathsf{X}^{be_{i}})\lambda_{a,b}\left|\psi_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle=(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{9}U^{e_{i}})\left|\psi_{\mathsf{L}}\right\rangle
.
The frameability of

P\triangleq\sum_{a,b}\lambda_{a,b}P^{(4)}_{a,b}

follows from the construction.

Finally, we prove
P\mkern 3.0mu\mathpunct{:}\allowbreak\mkern 3.0mu\mathop{\mathsf{prob}}1
.
19
19

19

This is quite trivial if we know that
\mathsf{ShorEC}
is loop-free, because its termination probability is clearly
1
.
However, here we prove
P\mkern 3.0mu\mathpunct{:}\allowbreak\mkern 3.0mu\mathop{\mathsf{prob}}1
even without depending on that knowledge, leveraging orthogonality.

Since the coefficients
\lambda_{a,b}
satisfy

\sum_{a,b\in\{0,1\}}\lvert\lambda_{a,b}\rvert^{2}=1
,
it suffices to prove
P^{(4)}_{a,b}\mkern 3.0mu\mathpunct{:}\allowbreak\mkern 3.0mu\mathop{\mathsf{prob}}1
and
that the assertions
\lparen P^{(4)}_{a,b}\rparen_{a,b\in\{0,1\}}
are orthogonal.
To begin with,
P^{(4)}_{a,b}
has the following form:

\displaystyle P^{\,\mathsf{BitEC}^{\textit{{\lgrfont{}}},\textit{{\lgrfont{}}}}}_{e,e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\ \triangleq\ \textstyle\bigoplus\nolimits^{\textit{{\lgrfont{}}},\textit{{\lgrfont{}}}}\delta_{\textit{{\lgrfont{}}},e\veebar e^{\prime}}\delta_{\textit{{\lgrfont{}}},e^{\prime}\veebar e^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle P^{(4)}_{a,b}

\displaystyle\ \triangleq\ P^{\,\mathsf{BitEC}^{\textit{{\lgrfont{}}}_{1},\textit{{\lgrfont{}}}_{1}}}_{ae_{1},ae_{2},ae_{3}}\,*\,P^{\,\mathsf{BitEC}^{\textit{{\lgrfont{}}}_{2},\textit{{\lgrfont{}}}_{2}}}_{ae_{4},ae_{5},ae_{6}}\,*\,P^{\,\mathsf{BitEC}^{\textit{{\lgrfont{}}}_{3},\textit{{\lgrfont{}}}_{3}}}_{ae_{7},ae_{8},ae_{9}}\,*\,P^{\,\mathsf{BitEC}^{\textit{{\lgrfont{}}}_{4},\textit{{\lgrfont{}}}_{4}}}_{b(e_{1}\lor e_{2}\lor e_{3}),b(e_{4}\lor e_{5}\lor e_{6}),b(e_{7}\lor e_{8}\lor e_{9})}\,*\,Q

Here,
Q
is some proposition satisfying
\mathsf{frameable}
and
\mathop{\mathsf{prob}}1
.
We can easily prove
P^{(4)}_{a,b}\mkern 3.0mu\mathpunct{:}\allowbreak\mkern 3.0mu\mathop{\mathsf{prob}}1
.
Now we prove the orthogonality of
\lparen P^{(4)}_{a,b}\rparen_{a,b\in\{0,1\}}
.
We can easily prove the orthogonality

P^{\,\mathsf{BitEC}^{\textit{{\lgrfont{}}},\textit{{\lgrfont{}}}}}_{\bar{e}}\perp P^{\,\mathsf{BitEC}^{\textit{{\lgrfont{}}},\textit{{\lgrfont{}}}}}_{\bar{e}^{\prime}}
for distinct
(\bar{e}),(\bar{e}^{\prime})\in\{0,1\}^{3}
.
So, for any two distinct parameters
(a,b),(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in\{0,1\}^{2}
,
at least one of the first four conjuncts in
P^{(4)}
is orthogonal.
20
20

20

Recall that we assume
e_{1}+\cdots+e_{9}=1
.
So exactly one of
e_{1},\ldots,e_{9}
is non-zero.
Also, exactly one of
e_{1}\lor e_{2}\lor e_{3}
,
e_{4}\lor e_{5}\lor e_{6}
and
e_{7}\lor e_{8}\lor e_{9}
is non-zero.

Therefore, by the rule
A.7
in
Section

A.7
,
the set
\{P^{(4)}_{a,b}\}_{a,b\in\{0,1\}}
is proved orthogonal.

B.7.
Probabilistic Choice and Almost Sure Termination

Here we give the details of
Section

5.6
.

Recall the repeat-until-success program we considered:

\mathsf{cointoss}^{\prime}_{p}\ \ \triangleq\ \ \mathsf{while}\mkern 6.0mu{\textsf{{a}}}\mkern 6.0mu\mathsf{do}\mkern 6.0mu{\big\lparen\mkern 2.0mu\mathsf{coin}^{\textsf{{a}}}_{p};\,\textsf{{c}}\mathbin{\text{\hbox{\hbox to7pt{\vbox to3.67pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{{}{}{}{}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@discardpath@clipped{M 0 0 L 0 5.08 L 9.69 5.08 L 9.69 0 Z} {{{}{}{{}}{}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}\textsf{{c}}+1\mkern 2.0mu\big\rparen}\hskip 30.00005pt\mathsf{cointoss}_{p}\ \ \triangleq\ \ \mathsf{coin}^{\textsf{{a}}}_{p};\ \mathsf{cointoss}^{\prime}_{p}.

We can prove the following specification for any probability
p\in\lparen 0,1]
, guaranteeing almost sure termination:

\exists\mkern 1.0mu\,P\mkern 3.0mu\mathpunct{:}\allowbreak\mkern 3.0mu\mathsf{frameable},\mathop{\mathsf{prob}}1.\allowbreak\,\ \big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\mkern 2.0mu\textsf{{c}}\mapsto 0\mkern 2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}^{\textsf{{a}}}\mkern 5.0mu{\mathsf{cointoss}_{p}}\mkern 5.0mu\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\mkern 2.0mu\textsf{{a}}\mapsto 0\mkern 2.0mu*\mkern 2.0muP\mkern 2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}.

Let
P_{n}\,\triangleq\,\sqrt{(1-p)^{n}}\mkern 1.0mu\cdot\mkern 1.0mu(\textsf{{a}}\mapsto 0\oplus_{p}\textsf{{a}}\mapsto 1)\mkern 1.0mu*\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{{c}}\mapsto n
,

Q_{n}\,\triangleq\,\sqrt{(1-p)^{n}p}\mkern 1.0mu\cdot\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{{a}}\mapsto 0\mkern 1.0mu*\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{{c}}\mapsto n
,
and
R_{n}\,\triangleq\,\sqrt{(1-p)^{n+1}}\mkern 1.0mu\cdot\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{{a}}\mapsto 1\mkern 1.0mu*\mkern 1.0mu\textsf{{c}}\mapsto n
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Also, we set
P\,\triangleq\,\bigoplus^{\textsf{{c}}}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sqrt{(1-p)^{n}p}
.
Then we can conclude the proof by the following derivation:

\hskip-27.74982pt\frac{\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\textsf{{c}}\mapsto 0}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}^{\textsf{{a}}}\mkern 5.0mu{\mathsf{coin}^{\textsf{{a}}}_{p}}\mkern 5.0mu\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{P_{0}}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}\hskip-6.43999pt\hskip 8.04996pt\frac{\frac{\forall\mkern 1.0mun.\allowbreak\,\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{P_{n}}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}\mkern 5.0mu{\textsf{{a}}}\mkern 5.0mu\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{\mkern 2.0mu{\uparrow}\mkern 1.0mu0*Q_{n}\mkern 1.0mu\oplus\mkern 1.0mu{\uparrow}\mkern 1.0mu1*R_{n}\mkern 2.0mu}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}\hskip-3.6pt\hskip 4.5pt\forall\mkern 1.0mun.\allowbreak\,\big\{\mkern 2.0mu{R_{n}}\mkern 2.0mu\big\}\mkern 5.0mu{\mathsf{coin}^{\textsf{{a}}}_{p};\,\textsf{{c}}\mathbin{\text{\hbox{\hbox to3.15pt{\vbox to1.65pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{{}{}{}{}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@discardpath@clipped{M 0 0 L 0 2.28 L 4.36 2.28 L 4.36 0 Z} {{{}{}{{}}{}

P\mkern 3.0mu\mathpunct{:}\allowbreak\mkern 3.0mu\mathsf{frameable},\mathop{\mathsf{prob}}1
holds.
The counter
c
is crucial for the unambiguity
P\mkern 3.0mu\mathpunct{:}\allowbreak\mkern 3.0mu\mathsf{unambig}
.
The probability judgment
P\mkern 3.0mu\mathpunct{:}\allowbreak\mkern 3.0mu\mathop{\mathsf{prob}}1
holds because
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(1-p)^{n}p\mkern 1.0mu=\mkern 1.0mu1
